# Build status

Tracker for `BUILD_CHUNKS.md` (final, 26 September 2026). Every development session reads this first and updates it last. `tools/next_chunk.py` (from C0a) edits the Status, Started, Finished, Commit, and Exit checks cells; sessions write Notes and the session log by hand. Status values: `TODO`, `IN_PROGRESS`, `DONE`, `BLOCKED`, `GATED`.

Every non-OUT goalie of such a team is listed (the pair is atomic: a pair that does not fit the slots left is skipped and the next pair is tried). Players the portfolio holds, and the existing DTD and portfolio-goalie reasons, keep today's order and come first; unresolved pairs fill the remaining slots by soonest game start, then team, then name. `qa.yaml` research.max_players stays 25. When the role state cannot be built, or the stored page store is empty, every slate team with a non-OUT goalie counts as unresolved (over-asking is bounded by the cap). The request names the unresolved teams that did not fit in `unresolved_goalie_teams_not_listed`. | 51 | How hard the own-goalie rule is, and what relaxes it (C20, B45, flag 13) | Written 2026-10-09 before any C20 code. The rule (no skater who plays against the lineup's own goalie) is a linear row per goalie team in `milp.LineupModel`, OFF unless a caller asks (`avoid_own_goalie`), so the FIELD sampler that shares the class is never touched. It is hard in candidate generation for every family of a groups menu. In selection, an entry of a rule family (large_gpp, small_field, wta; `config/risk.yaml` own_goalie.families) takes a compliant candidate. Relaxation order for one entry: compliant at cap levels 0 to 2, then any candidate at levels 0 to 2 (recorded as a Relaxation of kind OWN_GOALIE), then compliant at level 3 (goalie and game caps relaxed by the smallest step), then any candidate at level 3, then level 4 (repeat, every cap dropped), compliant candidates first and any candidate last. So the goalie and game caps and the risk budget are held ahead of the rule, a conflicted lineup is taken before a duplicate one, and the last attempt accepts every candidate, so no entry is left without a pick (refined 2026-10-09 while building: level 4 tries compliant first). In re-solves (late swap, controller) the rule is dropped only after every rule-on step of the ladder returns infeasible or a rule-on step errors or times out (a time limit is not infeasibility, but the rule-blind feasibility fallback must not be reached first), and the final lineups are audited, because the feasibility fallback has no rule. Kill switch: `own_goalie.enabled` | `build/own_goalie.py`, `build/milp.py`, `build/candidates.py`, `build/portfolio.py`, `config/risk.yaml` (C20) |

NOT COVERED, each for a reason: the Phase A baseline (v1) and its bank, which are built from local files before contest families are known (families resolve after Phase B); v1 reports `OWN_GOALIE=NOT_EVALUATED(...)` with the count of its lineups that face their own goalie, as information (the provisional pass, once it knows the families, adds a second bank of the same size built with the rule so a rule-family entry has compliant candidates, and selection filters the rest; refined 2026-10-09 while building: the Phase A bank is about two thirds conflicted, so a filter alone left 3 of 40 provisional entries conflicted); the field sampler (it models other people, who face their own goalie 11.7 percent of the time); Showdown (flag 53); cash and satellite (exempt, flag 13). Pins: the rule NEVER changes a pinned cell (locked or inside the edit stop); a conflict the pins force (a pinned goalie and a pinned skater against him) is allowed and named in the report line; late swap near lock does not treat an existing conflict as a reason to repair an entry (minimal change), so a conflicted v1 lineup in a rule family is reported, not rewritten. Family source in re-solves: the scenario cache, then the run manifest's provisional entries, then a contest-name pattern only when the name matched one (never the config default large_gpp); with none of them the rule is not applied and the line says NOT_EVALUATED(family unknown). Report line (it lives in the run's statuses, RUN_NOTES and the CLI output; the referee module, which checks the file, is unchanged): OWN_GOALIE=OK, FORCED_BY_PINS(k), RELAXED(k), OFF, NOT_APPLICABLE(Showdown) or NOT_EVALUATED(reason), with counts per family; a pool with no game information reports NOT_EVALUATED, never a quiet pass. | 57 | The C21 accept rule, as numbers (B44) | Written 2026-10-10 before any C21 run. A challenger (5-2-1 or 3-3-2) PASSES a slate if its mean gain over 4-3-1 is at least 2.78 standard errors AND at least 3 percent of the 4-3-1 mean AND its best-10 gain is above zero; it FAILS at a gain of 0 or less or under 1 percent; else INCONCLUSIVE. The minimum detectable effect (2.78 x the larger challenger standard error) is stated first and above 10 percent of the 4-3-1 mean makes the slate INCONCLUSIVE. ACCEPT-TO-SHADOW only if the same challenger passes BOTH real slates; REJECT if every challenger fails on at least one; a run that includes the synthetic bed is NOT MEASURED on real caches. INCONCLUSIVE, NOT MEASURED and NO VERDICT all count as not promoted for shipping, and the sleeve ships off or not at all. This adds one condition to the session-prompt default (the best-10 gain) and replaces its baseline, "the shape shipping today", with the fixed 4-3-1 core: the unconstrained search lands mostly in shapes outside the four, so a count of published lineups is unstable and could name 6-1-1, which the field does not contain | `docs/experiments/stack_size_2026-10-10.md` (C21) |

The cache keeps a body only after it parses, so the adapter also records every attempt (rotowire/attempts.json under the cache root), writing it before the request goes out, and sends no request within 600 s of any attempt, a failed, abandoned or overlapping one included; an attempt is one request (no retry); a failed request falls back to the stored copy, labeled with its age; an offline read never writes an attempt. | 86 | C54 which RotoWire statements count (B121) | Written 2026-10-12 before any run that tests it. Start: only an explicit start (will start, confirmed as the starter, will be in goal, gets the start), as whole phrases; a negation (will not start, won't start) never counts. Expected, probable, likely or projected is at most EXPECTED. Scratch: only an explicit absence from that game (scratched, will not play, won't play, ruled out, out for the game, tonight or the weekday); a bare out, a game-time decision, questionable or day-to-day is never a scratch. A later item saying the same player will play, will return or is in the lineup cancels his scratch. An AHL assignment or AHL start is ignored. A statement does not count when one of the four words before it or its own text negates or hedges it (not, never, whether, if, unless, could, may, might, likely, expected and the like: has not been ruled out, unclear whether he will start, might not start), when a condition follows it (if, unless, pending), or when what follows is not the game (will start skating, will miss practice). The subject must be the item's player: his last name before the statement with no other name in between and no other first name in front of it (a namesake), he only while no other person was named since him, and a start whose object is a name (the Hurricanes will start X) counts only for X. Anything else is ignored and counted on the report line. (The negation, hedge and subject rules were tightened on the review of the first code commit, before any measurement.) (Amended 2026-10-12 after the second recording (15:05Z), before any test of it: RotoWire's own phrasing puts home or road before the net word ('Markstrom will defend the road net against the Sabres on Monday'; 'he'll be between the home pipes'), so home or road may stand before the net word in each listed goalie job; and indicating, suggesting, signaling, implying and hinting (and their other forms) are hedges, so 'the first goalie to leave the ice Monday ..., indicating he'll be between the home pipes' is an inference, never a start.) | `data/sources/rotowire.py` classify |

| 94 | C54 RotoWire items carry a date, not a time (B121) | Written 2026-10-12 after the first recording (12:58Z) and before any test that uses it; reviewed by the independent review agent standing in for /advisor. Every item on https://www.rotowire.com/hockey/news.php prints only a date (div.news-update__timestamp, "October 11, 2026"); nothing on the page gives an item's time; items are listed newest first, and each headline link ends in a news id that rises with each new item (595556 to 595580 on that page); the page shows 25 items and its Get More News button leads to the subscription page. Rules: (a) an item's day is its printed date, taken as the America/New_York date. The recaps of CAR @ PHI (started 2026-10-11 23:00Z by the NHL schedule, so posted after 01:00Z on 10-12) print October 11, so it is not a UTC date; which US zone is not known, so an item posted near midnight may carry the neighbouring date, which can only drop it. (b) A dated item counts only when its date is the slate game's New York date and the page was read at most 60 s after the run clock (a live read lands within seconds of it); a page read later than that, live or stored, as in a rehearsal or replay with an earlier clock, is ignored (skipped_after_clock); a date later than the read's own New York date is skipped and counted (skipped_future_date). (c) Its time on the board (the GOALIES time column, RUN_NOTES, the scratch lines) is when the page was read, labeled as such on each RotoWire row and on the RotoWire line; no publish time is invented. (d) Between RotoWire items, later means the later time, then the higher news id (page order when an item has no id): read_items hands the board its rows oldest first, so the board's later-wins rules (the later start wins, the last expected item stands, a cancel after a scratch withdraws it) and its check for a later RotoWire item expecting another goalie (time, then position among the rows) follow the page's order. (e) An item without a player or a readable date is skipped and counted (skipped_malformed, skipped_no_time); a page on which no item has both raises SourceSchemaError. Amends flag 87 (the printed date replaces the published time; the after-the-clock rule is (b)) and flag 89 (the meaning of later). Cost: the board cannot say when RotoWire posted an item, so how early RotoWire had a confirmation is not measurable from the page; and only the latest 25 items are visible, so a morning confirmation can scroll off before T-60. (Amended 2026-10-12 on the review of the reader commit c9861ca, before any measurement: (b) said 'read during this pass (live) or at most 60 s after the clock', which let a live read in a rehearsal with an earlier clock count later news; the live clause is dropped and a date after the read is skipped.) | `data/sources/rotowire.py`, `build/goalies.py` |

| 96 | C23 goalie gate: what the ceiling ranking top 3 means (B49, flag 12) | Written 2026-10-13 before any C23 code or number; reviewed by an independent review agent (the advisor tool was not loaded in the session). Ceiling = the 99th percentile of a person's simulated points over the design scenarios (one column per person; the Captain's 1.5x does not change the order), ranked among every player on the slate, skaters and goalies: rank = 1 + the number of players with a strictly higher ceiling, so a tie at 3rd is allowed. With `discovery.captain.goalie_gate.enabled` a goalie whose rank is above top_k (3) has his Captain row excluded from every scenario-pass discovery job, and bank or provisional candidates with that Captain are dropped; skater Captain rows are never barred and FLEX rows are untouched. The ranks and the would-be-barred goalies are reported on every Showdown run, switch on or off. The switch lives in `risk.yaml` beside C20's goalie term, not in `ownership.yaml` field.captain_rules as flag 12 names, because captain_rules models the field's Captain picks and editing it would change the opponent model. Limits: at small design counts the 99th percentile rests on a few draws; a goalie with a 50 percent chance to start keeps almost his whole 99th percentile, so the gate barely penalizes an unconfirmed starter (kept as flag 12 states it). SHIPS OFF; the baseline (v1), provisional (v2), late swap and QA re-solves are not reached | `config/risk.yaml` discovery.captain.goalie_gate, `build/captain.py`, `build/portfolio.discover`, `build/scenario_pass.py` (C23) |

Points added to every Captain row (skater and goalie) of every scenario-pass discovery job: weight x (ceiling / the slate's highest ceiling) x ln(100 / max(CPT own percent, own_floor_pct)), ceiling as in flag 96, CPT own = the Captain role's ownership in the biggest contest's sampled field (the `own` map C20's term reads). weight 1.5 and own_floor_pct 1.0, hand-set by arithmetic and never fitted (C12): for two top-ceiling players, 30 percent owned against 5 percent is worth 2.7 points, about one noise standard deviation (perturb_sd 2.0, times 1.5 on a Captain row); the largest spread is 1.5 x (ln 100 - ln 2.5) = 5.5 points (a top-ceiling Captain at the 1 percent floor against a 40 percent chalk one). Two deviations from the session prompt's default (log ownership alone): the ceiling factor comes from B49's proposed change (1.5x ceiling over CPT ownership), so a near-zero-owned punt with no ceiling gets almost nothing; and goalie Captain rows get the term too, because a skater-only term would penalize every goalie Captain and blur the two arms. Consequence written now: if the field model under-captains goalies the term favors goalie Captains, so every Showdown run reports the modeled field's goalie-Captain share beside the one observed figure (23 percent, 2026-10-02), and a slate where the leverage arm raises goalie-Captain entries over the off arm cannot count toward accepting the term. Per scenario, the chance of beating N independent draws from the weighted field with ties shared: ((b + t)^(N + 1) - b^(N + 1)) / ((N + 1) t), b^N when t = 0, with b and t the field weight shares strictly below and tied and N the opponents (the field weight sum). Field only; reported beside the exact estimate (1[nothing above] / (ties + 1)) everywhere. | 115 | C25 co-ceiling cause: the decision rule (B64) | Written 2026-10-16 before any C25 code or calibrate run and before any linemate outcome was computed (planning only checked that MoneyPuck line ids join to the skater ids: every forward matched on 2025-11-07, 2026-01-10 and 2026-04-16). On the held-out dates, each pair of forwards on one team who both played and both dressed in at least 50 scenarios (C6's pair set) is labeled by model line (the simulator's forward line, as in C6) and by actual line: a forward's primary line in that game is the MoneyPuck 5on5 forward line row with the most ice time that contains him (ties: the smaller line_id), and actual linemates share a primary line. A forward with no line row in that game is UNMATCHED and his pairs are counted apart, never as not linemates. Four groups by model and actual line: TT, TF, FT, FF. For each: the joint ratio (pairs where both had 2+ points, observed against the simulator's own probability), the marginal ratio (the members' single 2+ point games over the same pair-games, observed against expected) and the adjusted ratio, joint divided by marginal squared, so overrated top-line players are not mistaken for too much correlation; the rule reads the adjusted ratio. Intervals: 90% (percentiles 5 and 95) from 2,000 bootstrap resamples of game ids, joint over the groups, from numpy default_rng([20260929, 25]), separate from the report's own draws. Model lines miss the real linemates is named when FT's adjusted ratio is above 1 and TF's below 1, both intervals excluding 1. Within-line correlation too strong is named when TT's adjusted ratio is below 1 with its interval excluding 1. Both can be named; when neither holds, both are rejected at this sample. If FF's adjusted ratio is above 1 with its interval excluding 1, the report says the excess is not confined to line structure (a description, not a cause). Read on the clean-constants run; every group's counts are printed beside its ratios. Additions to Ben's default, named as changes: the marginal adjustment; a group with fewer than 10 expected joint events cannot satisfy a condition; if fewer than 90% of forward pair-games are matched, the result is INCONCLUSIVE (join), not a verdict. | sim/validate.py co_ceiling_cause; docs/calibration/2026-10-16.json `co_ceiling.cause` |
| 116 | C25 the 3+ point tail preregistration bar (B6) | Written 2026-10-16 before any C25 code or number. B6's unit-level Gamma factor (a mean-one random multiplier, per scenario, on one forward line's or PP unit's scoring weights) is judged on 10 test dates disjoint from the 10 design dates of this chunk's report. Test dates: from pick_dates' 2025-26 regular-season game days (after its 30-day skip), remove the design dates and every game day within 3 calendar days of one, then take 10 evenly spaced (numpy linspace(0, n - 1, 10), rounded); docs/experiments/tail_2026-10-16.md lists them before any is simulated. Constants for that test are clean, excluding design and test dates. The variance is set on the design dates only: grid 0.05, 0.10, 0.20, 0.35, 0.50; among grid values whose design points>=2 ratio stays within 0.05 of the factor-off value, the one whose design points>=3 ratio is closest to 1.0 (ties: the smaller variance); if none qualifies, the factor is rejected before the test dates run. One test run, seed 20260929, 1,500 scenarios per date. Pass: test-date points>=3 ratio with the factor inside 0.8 to 1.25 (B6's bar). Changed from Ben's default, named: points>=2 is compared with the same test dates without the factor (within 0.05), not with the design-date report, so like is compared with like. Added, named: no rate or PIT row that is ok without the factor becomes FLAG with it; no co-ceiling group's adjusted ratio (flag 115) with the factor sits further from 1 than its distance without the factor plus its 90% half-width without the factor; if the test dates without the factor already sit inside 0.8 to 1.25, the deficiency did not replicate and the factor is not shipped. No Gamma code in C25. | docs/experiments/tail_2026-10-16.md; C13 or a later experiment |
| 117 | C25 seeds and run protocol (B64, B98) | Written 2026-10-16 before any calibrate run. Seed 20260929 (report's shipped seed), 1,500 scenarios per date, calibrate --seasons 1, one scratch copy of data/features/history (sha256 per file recorded) read by every run through a write guard (no writes under data/, runs/, outputs/ or BACKLOG.md; network blocked) with the four scratch roots. A1 and A2: origin/master 2091aad twice; they must match exactly. B: the C25 tree with the shipped constants; must match A. D (information only): the C25 tree with the all-dates recomputed constants at full precision, so D against B is rounding in the shipped files. C: the C25 tree with the clean constants, the committed report; C against D is the held-out effect. Must match: as_of_dates, n_scenarios, coverage, pit, rates, team, unavailable, the co_ceiling keys that exist today (pairs, observed, expected, ratio, flag, control_pairs, control_observed, control_expected, control_ratio), deficiencies as kind, what and value, and for A and B also notes. Exempt: created_utc, wall_s, the co-ceiling deficiency's detail text and the new keys (constants, co_ceiling.linemates, co_ceiling.cause, a constants deficiency). A difference between A1 and A2 or between A and B stops the chunk to think. After the co-ceiling commit, B and C rerun on the final code and must reproduce their earlier rows. Added 2026-10-16 after A1 and A2, while B ran and before D and C existed (advisor): D2, run D again at seed 20260930, information only; it gives the Monte Carlo reshuffle scale of every row, so a move inside it is not called a held-out effect, and it decides nothing. | BUILD_STATUS.md session log; docs/calibration/2026-10-16.md |
| 118 | C25 co-ceiling floor: keep the preregistered verdict, or also name the line-identity cause? (B64, flag 115) | Answered by Ben 2026-10-16, not a default: keep the preregistered co-ceiling verdict as recorded (named: within-line correlation too strong); the line-identity evidence goes to C13 as a description, not a named cause. Written 2026-10-16 after run C2's co-ceiling numbers. Flag 115 added a floor that was not in Ben's default: a group with fewer than 10 expected joint events cannot satisfy a condition. That floor kept "model lines miss the real linemates" from being named: actual linemates on different model lines (FT) ran at 2.91 adjusted (90% interval 2.18 to 3.71; 26 observed against 9.4 expected, under the floor) and pairs on one model line that were not actual linemates (TF) at 0.28 (0.16 to 0.41; 8 against 32.0). Ben's default without the added floor would have named both causes. The floor binds hardest on FT by construction: the simulator treats pairs it splits as nearly independent, so their expected joint count is small. Default in force: the preregistered verdict stands (named: within-line correlation too strong, TT adjusted 0.60, 0.47 to 0.75, 36 against 47.8), and the line-identity evidence goes to C13 as a description, not a named cause. Answer by telling a session: keep it, or name both (no rerun is needed; the numbers are in docs/calibration/2026-10-16.json). | C13's gate; docs/calibration/2026-10-16.json `co_ceiling.cause` |
| 119 | C41 the ownership error band (B74) | Written 2026-10-16 before any C41 code or replay; reviewed by the advisor tool on the draft plan, approved by Ben as a plan. The band is the concentration slope of actual on forecast ownership (OLS on deviations from each contest's mean, every graded role, zeros included), pooled per forecast over its large_gpp contests. beta_low = the smallest pooled slope among the graded Classic forecasts (09-30 and 09-29); beta_high = 1 / beta_low (the mirror in log space). The base forecast stays the center and is not retuned (card); every graded contest was flatter than its forecast (slopes 0.72 to 0.85, seen in planning and disclosed in the preregistration), which goes to a backlog row for C12. Classic only: Showdown's report prints SKIPPED and a backlog row holds its band. The per-player residual (sd about 5.5 points on 09-30) is not modeled | `docs/experiments/ownership_band_2026-10-16.md`, `models/ownership.py` `error_band`, `tests/fixtures/c41/graded_ownership.csv` (C41) |
| 120 | C41 the low and high fields and their calibration (B74) | Written 2026-10-16 before any C41 code or replay. One temperature t multiplies every behavior's Gumbel noise sd with every seed unchanged (t = 1 is today's field byte for byte), applied to the provisional draws and the growth draws on the run's own feature table. On each replay pool (09-29 and 09-30), bisection on log t over [0.25, 4], at most 12 steps, to a realized slope (the large_gpp field's role ownership at t on the same field's at t = 1) within 0.02 of beta_low and of beta_high; an unreachable target takes the bound and the decision is read there. `config/ownership.yaml` `field.error_band` records the geometric mean of the two pools' t per side as a PRIOR. Fallback only if noise cannot reach a target and implied_total is non-zero on the pool: t also divides the stack-team log-weights | `docs/experiments/ownership_band_2026-10-16.md`, `models/field.py`, `models/field_fast.py`, `config/ownership.yaml` (C41) |
| 121 | C41 does the spread move a pick: the rule (B74, B130) | Written 2026-10-16 before any C41 code or replay. Replays of 20260929-222125-classic and 20260930-210607-classic under the C55 protocol (flag 111), seed 20261015, 30 cloned entries, goal first_place. At the chosen knob each entry is scored under each alternative field with the state advanced by the base fill's earlier picks (anchored), candidates the union of the base screen and that field's screen. Controls: t = 1 with field seeds + 1 and + 2 (provisional and growth draws both reseeded). Large_gpp picks only. Moves iff, on both slates, max(low, high) anchor flips > max(control 1, control 2) anchor flips. Tie criterion (B130): a pick flip without an anchor flip is reported, never counted; per pick, which of the 3% floor and one SE set the band. Known bias: low and high reuse the base uniforms, so the rule leans toward no move. On moves: `selection.ownership_band` (mean of the three fields' scores) is built and ships OFF, adoption waits on C51; otherwise no selection code | `docs/experiments/ownership_band_2026-10-16.md`, `scripts/c41_ownership_band.py` (C41) |
| 122 | C41 the spread report in every run (B74) | Written 2026-10-16 before any C41 code or replay. After the scenario pass publishes, read-only: low and high fields at the config's t, built and freed one at a time; every candidate's spread in `runs/<id>/scenario/ownership_bands.json`, a manifest summary, one RUN_NOTES line naming anchored flips; its own time budget; a failure is a report line and never costs the file. Ships ON only if the replays show at most 60 s added wall time and at most 1 GB added peak memory at 30 entries on both slates; otherwise `field.error_band.report: false` | `config/ownership.yaml` `field.error_band.report`, `build/scenario_pass.py`, `build/notes.py` (C41) |
| 123 | C41 the switch-off selection rule's design (B74, flag 121) | Written 2026-10-16 after flag 121's rule fired (09-29 29 > 23, 09-30 21 > 19) and before any switch code or switch replay; reviewed by the advisor tool. `config/risk.yaml` `selection.ownership_band` ships false. On: per scenario the step utility and the lose-80% indicator averaged over three contest books (base, low and high fields at the config's t, each with its own running payout), score = mean utility - kappa x fees x mean indicator, SE paired; candidates the union of the three screens; tie order the base field's ownership and duplicates; the pick placed in all three books, caps and counts the base fill's; the knob, frontier and joint recount on the base field. Off: no extra field, no prepared context, bytes unchanged. Adoption waits on C51 (B112); its replay is information read beside part 2's control range | `docs/experiments/ownership_band_2026-10-16.md` part 3, `build/portfolio.py`, `build/scenario_pass.py`, `config/risk.yaml` (C41) |
